# Proposed OEIS comments: A125389 to A183357

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A125389

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A125389>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [722](../papers/00501-01000/00722-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> [Empirical] a(base,n)=a(base-1,n)+F(5) for base>=5.int(n/2)+1 and F(d) is the largest coefficient in (1+x+...+x^(2d))^n

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This identity is true. Both sides have a generating function that the entries themselves record, and after clearing denominators the difference is identically zero, which is a finite polynomial check. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A126089

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A126089>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [12604](../papers/12501-13000/12604-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: (-n+4)*a(n) +2*(2*n-5)*(n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 24 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A126501

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A126501>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12602](../papers/12501-13000/12602-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> [Empirical] a(base,n) = a(base-1,n)+9^(n-1) for base>=4n-3; a(base,n) = a(base-1,n)+9^(n-1)-2 when base=4n-4.
> Conjecture: a(n) = (2^(-1-n)*((5-sqrt(41))^n*(-7+sqrt(41)) + (5+sqrt(41))^n*(7+sqrt(41)))) / sqrt(41). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 20 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured closed form is correct. The entry states a generating function as fact; a linear recurrence with polynomial coefficients follows from it exactly, the conjectured form satisfies the same recurrence, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to be equal from then on. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A127905

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A127905>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11447](../papers/11001-11500/11447-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

```

```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A129365

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A129365>  
Result obtained: **23 August 2026**  
Paper: [5](../papers/00001-00500/00005-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures:
> B) If p is a prime then p|a(n) if and only if p <= n/3. Let ordp(n,p) denote the exponent of the largest power of p which divides n. For example, ordp(48,2) = 4 since 48 = 3*(2^4). The precise decomposition of a(n) into primes would follow from the following two conjectures:
> Adamczewski provides 2 proof abstracts addressing Conjectures B and C in linked article. - _Michael De Vlieger_, Aug 20 2026

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Conjecture A is true, and more: for every prime p, ord_p(a(n)) = Sum_{i>=1} B(floor(n/p^i)) with B(M) = Sum_{k=1..M} (M mod k) = A004125(M). A sum of remainders is nonnegative, so every exponent is, which gives A; the formula is Conjecture D, and B and C follow from it as well. The proof is the known factorisation of the numerator, the identity floor(n/(k*p^i)) = floor(floor(n/p^i)/k) for the denominator, and M^2 - Sum_{k<=M} k*floor(M/k) = Sum_{k<=M} (M mod k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A129454

Result obtained: **23 August 2026**

Then we conjecture that the prime factorization of a(n) is given by ordp(a(n),p)=(floor(n/p))^3 + (floor(n/p^2))^3 + (floor(n/p^3))^3 + . . .. Compare with the comments in A092287.

## A129833

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A129833>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [4357](../papers/04001-04500/04357-PROOF.pdf), [4871](../papers/04501-05000/04871-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(n+1)*a(n) -n*(2*n+5)*a(n-1) +(n-1)*(n^2+6*n+3)*a(n-2) -(n-2)*(3*n^2-2)*a(n-3) +(n-2)*(n-3)*(3*n-4)*a(n-4) -(n-4)*(n-3)^2*a(n-5) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 28 2015
> Conjecture: (n+1)*(n^2-4*n+2)*a(n) -n*(2*n^3-5*n^2-6*n+3)*a(n-1) +n*(n-1)*(n^3-2*n^2-2*n-2)*a(n-2) -(n-2)*(n^2-2*n-1)*(n-1)^2*a(n-3) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 28 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A140901

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A140901>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [8534](../papers/08501-09000/08534-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> (Empirical) Set p,q,r to n,5,3 (in any order) in s=p+q+r-1; a(n) = Product_{i=0..r-1} (binomial(s,p+i)*i!/(s-i)^(r-i-1)).
> (Conjecture) G.f.: (1 + 40*x + 400*x^2 + 1456*x^3 + 2212*x^4 + 1456*x^5 + 400*x^6 + 40*x^7 + x^8)/(1-x)^16. - _Bruno Berselli_, May 08 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical formula is true. Reverse the rows and the columns: a matrix over 0..n that is nondecreasing along rows and down columns becomes one that is nonincreasing, which is exactly a plane partition with parts at most n inside a P X Q rectangle -- so a(n) counts the plane partitions in a P X Q X n box, which is the formula already noted on this entry. That triple product telescopes in its third index, leaving a(n) = Product_{i=1..P} Product_{j=1..Q} (n+i+j-1)/(i+j-1), a polynomial in n of degree P*Q. Clearing the denominator of the empirical expression then turns it into an identity between two polynomials whose degrees are bounded by inspection, so checking it at that many points settles it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Reverse the rows and the columns: a matrix over 0..n that is nondecreasing along rows and down columns becomes one that is nonincreasing, which is exactly a plane partition with parts at most n inside a P X Q rectangle -- so a(n) counts the plane partitions in a P X Q X n box, which is the formula already noted on this entry. That triple product telescopes in its third index, leaving a(n) = Product_{i=1..P} Product_{j=1..Q} (n+i+j-1)/(i+j-1), a polynomial in n of degree P*Q. Clearing the denominator of the empirical expression then turns it into an identity between two polynomials whose degrees are bounded by inspection, so checking it at that many points settles it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A140935>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [6718](../papers/06501-07000/06718-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> (Empirical) set p,q,r to n,11,3 (in any order) in s=p+q+r-1; a(n) = product {i in 0..r-1} (binomial(s,p+i)*i!/(s-i)^(r-i-1))

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical formula is true. Reverse the rows and the columns: a matrix over 0..n that is nondecreasing along rows and down columns becomes one that is nonincreasing, which is exactly a plane partition with parts at most n inside a P X Q rectangle -- so a(n) counts the plane partitions in a P X Q X n box, which is the formula already noted on this entry. That triple product telescopes in its third index, leaving a(n) = Product_{i=1..P} Product_{j=1..Q} (n+i+j-1)/(i+j-1), a polynomial in n of degree P*Q. Clearing the denominator of the empirical expression then turns it into an identity between two polynomials whose degrees are bounded by inspection, so checking it at that many points settles it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A141135

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A141135>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [2120](../papers/02001-02500/02120-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Apr 05 2019: (Start)
> Conjecture: if n is a term in A121149, a(n) = a(n-1) + 3, otherwise a(n) = a(n-1) + 4. - _Jinyuan Wang_, Apr 05 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Both of these conjectures are false, and the entry's own terms refute them. The generating function reproduces a(1)..a(23) and then disagrees at n = 24, 27 and 30; the recurrence holds up to n = 23 and fails at n = 24 and again at n = 25. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A143017

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A143017>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [12607](../papers/12501-13000/12607-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -8*n*(n+1)*a(n) + 4*n*(2*n+5)*a(n-1) + 4*n*(n+7)*a(n-2) + 2*(70*n^2-395*n+564)*a(n-3) + 2*(25*n^2-143*n+222)*a(n-4) + 4*(49*n-228)*(n-5)*a(n-5) - 45*(n-5)*(n-6)*a(n-6) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 14 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A157713

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A157713>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [12600](../papers/12501-13000/12600-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*a(n) -4*(2*n+1)*(2*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A158690

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A158690>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [84](../papers/00001-00500/00084-PROOF.pdf), [85](../papers/00001-00500/00085-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures:
> Conjectural g.f.: G(exp(t)) as a formal power series in t, where G(q) := Sum_{n >= 0} q^(2*n+1) * Product_{k = 1..2*n} (1 - q^k). - _Peter Bala_, May 16 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, for the sequence and for every shift of it. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; the operator (1 + t) d/dt carries that property to every shift, and integrality then reduces a_i(n) mod p^r to an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, for which the Gauss congruences are classical. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A162314

Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [86](../papers/00001-00500/00086-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: for fixed k = 1,2,..., the sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the exact period dividing phi(k), where phi(k) is the Euler totient function A000010. For example, modulo 24 the sequence becomes [1, 4, 0, 16, 0 16, 0, 16, ...] with an apparent period of 2 beginning at a(2). - _Peter Bala_, Jul 08 2022
> Conjectural o.g.f. as a continued fraction of Stieltjes type: 1/(1 - 4*x/(1 - 2*x/(1 - 8*x/(1 - 4*x/(1 - ... - 3*n*x/(1 - 2*n*x/(1 - ...))))))). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A162972

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A162972>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11644](../papers/11501-12000/11644-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(-3*n+8)*a(n-1) +(n-3)*(3*n-14)*a(n-2) +(-n^3+19*n^2-98*n+156)*a(n-3) +(-4*n^3+56*n^2-258*n+395)*a(n-4) +(-6*n^3+84*n^2-395*n+624)*a(n-5) -(n-5)*(4*n^2-41*n+106)*a(n-6) -(n-5)*(n-6)^2*a(n-7)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A163733

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A163733>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [2178](../papers/02001-02500/02178-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + a(n-2) for n >= 5.
> G.f.: (1-x^3)/(1-x-x^2) (conjecture). - _Paul Barry_, Aug 10 2009
> a(n) = round(phi^(k-1)) - round(phi^(k-1)/sqrt(5)), phi = (1 + sqrt(5))/2 (conjecture). - _Federico Provvedi_, Mar 26 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 3*a(n-2) + a(n-3) for n>=9.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 26 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A166762

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A166762>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [1554](../papers/01501-02000/01554-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 11*a(n-1) - 53*a(n-2) + 147*a(n-3) - 259*a(n-4) + 301*a(n-5) - 231*a(n-6) + 113*a(n-7) - 32*a(n-8) + 4*a(n-9).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 66 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A167137

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A167137>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [87](../papers/00001-00500/00087-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> CONJECTURE: Sum_{n>=0} a(n)^m * log(1+x)^n/n! is an integer series in x for all integer m>0; see A167138 and A167139 for examples.
> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 16 we obtain the sequence [1, 1, 5, 15, 1, 7, 1, 7, 1, 7, ...], with an apparent period of 2 beginning at a(4).
> More generally, we conjecture that the same property holds for integer sequences having an e.g.f. of the form G(exp(x) - 1), where G(x) is an integral power series. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A172025

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A172025>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [11609](../papers/11501-12000/11609-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 2*n*(n+3)*a(n) + (-7*n^2 - 17*n - 8)*a(n-1) -2*(n+2)*(2*n+1)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 19 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +(2-7n)*a(n-1) +3(17-7n)*a(n-2) +9(11n-34)*a(n-3) +108(4-n)*a(n-4) +36(n-5)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 27 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A180752

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A180752>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [10802](../papers/10501-11000/10802-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - a(n-3) - a(n-5).
> Empirical: G.f.: -x^2*(-1+x+x^3+x^2) / ( (x-1)*(1+x)*(x^3+2*x-1) ). a(n)-a(n-2) = A052980(n-3). - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

## A181255

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A181255>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [10174](../papers/10001-10500/10174-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> The number of 1s in each row repeats with period 3, and we can divide the matrices into 12 classes (013, 022, 031, 103, 112, 121, 130, 202, 211, 220, 301, or 310) based on the pattern of row sums. The number of matrices in each class satisfies b(n) = 3*b(n-1), 3*b(n-3), or 9*b(n-3), depending on the number of 1s and 2s in the pattern. Therefore, the combined sequence satisfies [(T - 3I)(T^3 - 3I)(T^3 - 9I)](a)(n) = 0, where T is the right shift operator defined by T(a)(n) = a(n+1), and I is the identity operator. This is equivalent to the empirical formula for a(n) given below. - _David Radcliffe_, Jan 12 2023
> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 12*a(n-3) - 36*a(n-4) - 27*a(n-6) + 81*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 6*x*(21 - 7*x - 17*x^2 - 291*x^3 + 45*x^4 + 99*x^5 + 756*x^6) / ((1 - 3*x)*(1 - 3*x^3)*(1 - 9*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 26 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is a condition on every subblock of a fixed size, so a bounded window of consecutive lines decides it, and counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 12 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183314

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183314>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [11143](../papers/11001-11500/11143-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 3*a(n-2) - a(n-3) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 3*x - 2*x^2 - x^3) / (1 - x - 3*x^2 + x^3 + x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183355

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183355>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [11604](../papers/11501-12000/11604-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n)=3*a(n-1) for n>2

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 3 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A183356

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183356>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [11428](../papers/11001-11500/11428-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - a(n-3) for n>4.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 28 2018: (Start)
> Assuming Colin Barker's conjectures, a(n) = (12*Fibonacci(n+1))^2, n>1. - _Ehren Metcalfe_, Apr 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
